\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l\geqslant 2}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{l!}\biggl[\partial_{L-2}\Bigl(\dot{I}_{ijL-2}(u)\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\frac{2l}{l+1}\partial_{aL-2}\Bigl(\epsilon_{ab(i}J_{j)bL-2}(u)\Bigr)\biggr],

This form of
\Box^{-1}\left[\underline{\bar{h}}_{\mu\nu}\right]
implies that :

\begin{split}\partial^{\nu}\Box^{-1}\left[\underline{\bar{h}}_{0\nu}\right]&=\frac{1}{2}I,\\
\partial^{\nu}\Box^{-1}\left[\underline{\bar{h}}_{i\nu}\right]&=-\frac{1}{2}\dot{I}_{i}+\epsilon_{iab}\partial_{a}\left(\mathchoice{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}{J^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{b}}}}\right).\\
\end{split}

(42)

And
I
,
I_{i}
and
J_{b}
are constants, so they will disappear through the action of any differential operator.

A.5
Derivation of a STF multipole

We investigate how one can express the partial derivatives of a STF multipole
\{I_{L},J_{L}\}
in such a way that we can easily find the orders 0 and 1 of
(1/r)^{k}
.

Let
I
be a function of only the retarded time
t-r
, and let us calculate the first powers in
1/r
of
\partial_{L}\left(I\right)
and
\partial_{L}\left(r^{-1}I\right)
as :

\begin{split}\partial_{L}\left(I\right)&=\sum^{[l/2]}_{k=0}c_{k}\delta_{\{2K}\hat{\partial}_{L-2K\}}\Delta^{k}I\\
\end{split}

(43)

From this we have (provable by induction) :

\begin{split}\Delta^{k}\left(I\right)&=\partial_{0}^{2k}I-2k\partial_{0}^{2k-1}I,\\
\end{split}

(44)

and by inserting (
44
) into (
43
), we find :

\begin{split}\partial_{L}\left(I\right)&=\sum^{[l/2]}_{k=0}c_{k}\delta_{\{2K}\hat{\partial}_{L-2K\}}\left(\partial_{0}^{2k}I-2k\partial_{0}^{2k-1}\frac{I}{r}\right)\\
&=\sum^{[l/2]}_{k=0}c_{k}\delta_{\{2K}\partial^{2k}_{0}\biggl(\left[\frac{\hat{n}_{L-2K\}}}{(-2)^{l-2k}}\sum^{l-2k}_{n=1}e_{l,k,n}r^{n-l+2k}\partial_{0}^{n}I\right]\\
&-2k\partial_{0}^{-1}\left[(-1)^{l-2k}\hat{n}_{L-2K\}}\sum^{l-2k}_{n=0}f_{l,k,n}\partial_{0}^{l-2k-n}\frac{I}{r^{n+1}}\right]\biggr).\\
\end{split}

(45)

with :

e_{l,k,n}=\frac{2^{n}(2l-4k-n-1)!}{(n-1)!(l-2k-n)!}

f_{l,k,n}=\frac{(l-2k+n)!}{2^{n}n!(l-2k-n)!}

We only keep the
\mathcal{O}(1/r)
terms, so when
n=l-2k
and
n=l-2k-1
in the second sum, and
n=0
in the third sum of (
46
).

\begin{split}\partial_{L}\left(I\right)&=\sum^{[l/2]}_{k=0}c_{k,l}\delta_{\{2K}\partial^{2k}_{0}\biggl(\frac{\hat{n}_{L-2K\}}}{(-2)^{l-2k}}\Bigl[2^{l-2k}\partial_{0}^{l-2k}I\\
&+\frac{2^{l-2k-1}(l-2k)!}{(l-2k-2)!}r^{-1}\partial_{0}^{l-2k-1}I\Bigr]\\
&-2k\partial_{0}^{-1}\left[(-1)^{l-2k}\hat{n}_{L-2K\}}\partial_{0}^{l-2k}\frac{I}{r}\right]\biggr)+\mathcal{O}(1/r^{2}).\end{split}

(46)

Let us define
a_{k,l}
,
b_{k,l}
,
d_{k,l}
and
c_{k,l}
:

\displaystyle a_{k,l}=

\displaystyle c_{k,l}(-1)^{l},

(47)

\displaystyle b_{k,l}=

\displaystyle c_{k,l}(l-2k)(l-2k-1)\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2},

\displaystyle d_{k,l}=

\displaystyle 2kc_{k,l}(-1)^{l+1},

\displaystyle c_{k,l}=

\displaystyle\frac{(2l-4k+1)!!}{(2l-2k+1)!!},

and :

\begin{split}\partial_{L}\left(I\right)=&\sum^{[l/2]}_{k=0}\delta_{\{2K}\hat{n}_{L-2K\}}\left(a_{k}\partial_{0}^{l}I+\left(b_{k}+d_{k}\right)\partial_{0}^{l-1}\frac{I}{r}\right)\\
&+\mathcal{O}(1/r^{2}).\end{split}

(48)

Let us do the same for
I/r
:

\begin{split}\partial_{L}\left(I/r\right)=&\sum^{[l/2]}_{k=0}c_{k,l}\delta_{\{2K}\hat{\partial}_{L-2K\}}\partial_{0}^{2k}\left(\frac{I}{r}\right)\\
=&\sum^{[l/2]}_{k=0}c_{k}\delta_{\{2K}\hat{n}_{L-2K\}}(-1)^{l-2l}\partial_{0}^{2K}f_{l,k,0}\partial_{0}^{l-2k}\left(\frac{I}{r}\right)\\
&+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)\\
=&\sum^{[l/2]}_{k=0}a_{k}\delta_{\{2K}\hat{n}_{L-2K\}}\partial_{0}^{l}\left(\frac{I}{r}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\right)\\
\end{split}

(49)

&\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{6}}}}E^{{\kern-14.20667pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern p\leqslant 2}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{6}}}}B^{{\kern-14.33409pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.383pt{khP}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-8.9441pt{6}\kern 3.96252pt{khP}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-5.82364pt{6}\kern 3.96252pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.26962pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.26962pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.3548pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.68199pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{6}}}}B^{{\kern-8.54147pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.83037pt{khP}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-4.69148pt{6}\kern 2.83037pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.26962pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.26962pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.3548pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.68199pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{ij}}}}\sum^{[\frac{p+l}{2}]}_{q=0}\delta_{\{2Q}\hat{n}_{L+P-2Q\}}\left(b_{q,l}+d_{q,l}\right),\\
\end{split}

(51)

where the sequences
a_{q,l}
,
b_{q,l}
and
d_{q,l}
have the following expressions :

a_{q,l}=c_{q,l}(-1)^{l}

b_{q,l}=c_{q,l}(l-2q)(l-2q-1)\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2}

d_{q,l}=2qc_{q,l}(-1)^{l+1}

c_{q,l}=\frac{(2l-4q+1)!!}{(2l-2q+1)!!}

A.8
Unsuppressed terms

The terms unsuppressed by
1/r
in (
21
) come from a specific form of the source terms. Any gravitation theory whose linearised gravitational waves are described by an equation of the sort :

\begin{split}\Box\overstar{h}_{\mu\nu}=\sum_{l}\mathchoice{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}\partial_{\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\underline{h}_{\alpha_{\beta}}.\\
\end{split}

(52)

Where
\overstar{h}_{\mu\nu}
are beyond-GR metric perturbations, as in (
10
)
\underline{h}_{\alpha\beta}
is the 1-PM metric perturbation from General Relativity, and the
\mathchoice{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
are tensors that respect the identity
\partial_{\gamma}\mathchoice{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}=0
.
Under these conditions, the solution of (
52
), up to a homogeneous solution, is :

\begin{split}\overstar{h}_{\mu\nu}=\sum_{l}\mathchoice{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}{K^{{{\alpha\beta\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[32.29654pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.83047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[13.83978pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}}\partial_{\epsilon_{1}...\epsilon_{l}}\Box^{-1}\underline{h}_{\alpha_{\beta}}.\\
\end{split}

(53)

With
\Box^{-1}\underline{h}_{\alpha_{\beta}}
defined by (
17
). One can see immediately that a lot of terms unsuppressed by
1/r
and proportional to the time derivated multipoles {
I_{L}
,
J_{L}
} appear. And since these unsuppressed terms are not homogeneous solutions of the wave equations, they cannot be negated by adding a carefully chosen homogeneous solution.

Additionnaly, if one considers the gauge-moment laid-out in
Blanchet 2014
, they cannot be absorbed in a gauge transformation as well. Specifically, it means that any SME formalism subset with a vacuum value (for spontaneous breaking), or an explicit value (for explicit breaking), constant everywhere in some coordinate system would most likely suffer from the same pathology. For instance, the linearised equations in
Kostelecký and Mewes 2016
;
Kostelecký and Mewes 2018
would give rise to the same unsuppressed terms. One can apply the insights of this article to those cases and conclude that those SME subsets are heavily disfavored as well.
